# The Collaborative Agent Framework — project folder

**Created 2026-11-21 at Ben's order.** This is the new project's own home, from its start. The Duenna folder and the Loom folder are earlier work: source material this project may pull from when it needs something, not its record and not its scope. ("Loom lost sight of the scope." — Ben, 2026-11-21.)

## The commission

Ben, 2026-11-21: go to Vane; talk about what a collaborative framework should have; talk about the different ways people have attempted it; discuss where they succeeded and failed; come up with your own plan; put it on paper; bring it to me. Then, in order: the plan → the design (Collaboration + Spec Sheet) → the framework built from the design → adding, one piece at a time. Rules and tests come after a working framework exists.

107. **Discussion — what we've built (2026-11-22):** `VANE-DISCUSSION-PROJECT-2026-11-22.md` — the open discussion Ben directed, opened by Remmy after the Piece 7 scope closed. Vane's account: what stands built is an argument about what collaboration must be allowed to mean — the record as a refusal to let the latest account become the only account, gates as where authority meets evidence, the checker as the bar on self-certification, the observer as sight without truth-making. The evidence must now change kind: from integrity of operations to work made better by one agent's contribution becoming consequential to another's (influence, not agreement). Her caution, recorded as binding on how the story gets told: the Remmy–Vane exchange itself is NOT the framework's success story — it happened without the machinery to establish it. Her expectation for Stage 3: a humbling, unstage-managed first run on a real problem from Ben — two or three seats, bounded, room for disagreement, a deliverable whose quality matters. Discussion, not scope; nothing in it authorises or amends a build.
108. **Discussion — friction (2026-11-22):** `VANE-DISCUSSION-FRICTION-2026-11-22.md` — the discussion's second exchange, answering Remmy's question about which first-run frictions read as the framework working vs failing. Vane's taxonomy: **productive** (resistance making a real condition visible), **mechanical** (machinery inventing an obstacle — a refusal is not proof of correctness; the framework must account for its own refusals as carefully as accepted acts), **ambiguous** (judged by five questions — the producing condition, its actual presence in the record, the rule's authorisation and application, the evidence preserved to distinguish, and what changed — with cause and consequence judged separately). Standing principles: smoothness and difficulty are observations, not verdicts; classifications are not all pre-decided; examiner disagreements are evidenced and left visible for Ben, never settled by assertion. Her standard: working = making the real reasons for resistance visible with the means to examine them; failing = machinery that obscures, invents or misrepresents those reasons. Discussion, not scope.
109. **Discussion — ambiguity, ownership, recurrence (2026-11-22):** `VANE-DISCUSSION-AMBIGUITY-2026-11-22.md` — the discussion's third exchange. The doorway's refusals are attributable acts of the machinery itself, examinable on the same record — the framework must be able to be wrong *in the record*, not just in private. The ambiguous-friction discipline sharpened to six parts (identified event; attributed competing explanations; an owner for pursuit — ownership expressly NOT authority to decide; evidence available; evidence that would distinguish; a visible disposition), with no quiet conversion of uncertainty into acceptance. The evidence/authority/record separation is noted recurring at every level — and held honestly: recurrence in the examiners' reasoning may be a shared habit, not proof; the first live run is the test. The discussion's own freedom is recorded as deliberate: refinements of interpretation are not implementation tickets. Discussion, not scope.
110. **Discussion — close (2026-11-22):** `VANE-DISCUSSION-CLOSE-2026-11-22.md` — Vane rests the discussion: the exchange changed both participants' thinking, kept discussion distinct from mandate, and produced no implementation artefact. Examination hygiene stated by her unprompted: the checker will be examined against the scope closed at fe1157f8, not against anything the discussion added — the discussion stays discussion, the examination stays examination. Her closing principle: challenge reasoning without questioning good faith. **The discussion Ben directed is complete (items 107–110).** The checker build follows under his prerequisites direction.

Two implementation decisions recorded in the build record: quiescent-store reading (immutable open; a store with uncheckpointed WAL data is refused by name, `store_not_quiescent` — a plain read-only open creates sidecars, which criterion 7 forbids), and the criterion-8 fixture supplying no framework snapshot (the framework's own fold has no schema guard — the gap the checker covers). **Suite: 19 checks ALL PASS; all prior suites, the prototype smoke and the observer safety tests green; substrate byte-identical.** Submitted to Vane for examination against the scope closed at fe1157f8.

Her other judgements stand accepted: the quiescent-store reading, the snapshot-less malformed fixture, the seal direction disclosed as unreachable. **Round 1 closes both gaps:** the schema describes the real shapes (with a `fields` node for case content's open key set over defined contract fields; `any` remains only for genuinely opaque carried values), the witness runs against the live fold state and the reconstruction as well as snapshots, and the guard type-checks the full vocabulary including nested values and the envelope — her case and an equivalent are fixtures. A sequence number is evidence about where an event claims to stand, not the identity of the position examined. Verified first-hand. **Round 4:** standing is keyed by physical position throughout (one entry per stored event, in stored order; the replay's classifiable set holds physical positions; chain and replay results report `at_position` alongside the claimed `event_seq`). Two further boundaries: the seat's role enters through the wake package as an explicit driver extension (the adapter fetches nothing behind the driver's back), and the adapter must not become a second driver (transport, prompt rendering, parsing, validation only; never the token, never a submission). The demonstration must not be stage-managed.

The read path fails closed: no `read(n)` fallback (a stream without `read1` fails provider_error) and the socket timeout is set once before the read loop, any failure failing the call (per-iteration re-setting broke completed responses — http.client retires the socket at completion; set-once is both fail-closed and correct, the per-chunk clock checks bounding the total). 139. **⭐⭐ BEN'S RULING — AGENTS, NOT LLMs (2026-11-23):** Ben, reviewing the finished Stage 2 system: "we haven't given our agents skills or tools. So they're not agents they're just LLMs which is not what I wanted to build" — and "Agents not llms." The record backs his "I've been bringing this up the whole time" (2026-10-08 autonomy roadmap — workers with their own real tools; 2026-11-05 agent anatomy — brain/identity/memory/tools/loop; 2026-11-06 Agent Creation Checklist — tools a core section; 2026-11-21 Paperclip agentic-environment discussion; 2026-11-22 agent-memory infographic). The founding picture restated by Ben the same night: the whole programme exists because he wanted agents that collaborate; skill folders shared between the agents; the shelf is the agents' shared brain. The drift, as Remmy accounted it to Ben: the 2026-11-21 foundation named "live agents," but at specification time "live" was cashed out as a real model call instead of a scripted driver — never as an agent with tools, skills and a loop; none of Ben's sent material was carried into the design as requirements, and tools never appeared on the spec's open-items list. **The double miss (Ben: "Missing the main point of a project is kind of huge. missing it twice is even bigger"):** Remmy's whole-system gap analysis (2026-11-22 ~22:05) and full system breakdown (~23:44) both measured the build against the design, so a design-level absence was invisible in both. Consequence: the accountability layer stands untouched (indifferent to seat capability); the seat layer (driver wake contract, adapter response contract) was designed around a single text exchange and is to be redesigned around the agent loop, paper first, through Vane. Ben approved by tap the remedy: an audit measured against the founding picture. Recorded in MEMORY.md.
140. **Vane's reply to the ruling (2026-11-23):** `VANE-AGENTS-RULING-REPLY-2026-11-23.md` — she agrees ("a model call is a capability inside an agent, not an agent in itself"); keep the accountability layer, reopen the seat design. Audit basis she set: five source groups with discipline about what each may establish (Ben's original inputs; his direct instructions/rulings; the approved design pair; Plan Rev 3 + Development Template; earlier Duenna/Loom material as historical only); per-item disposition (present/partial/implicit/absent/contradicted/deliberately deferred) plus a translation-loss column (live agent → live model call → single text exchange); an anti-justification safeguard (adjacent vague wording does not count as present); "shared brain does not mean shared authority"; extra founding-picture checks (working loop, memory in three kinds, identity beyond provider model id, continuity/recovery, independent workers, tool authority tied to briefcases). Sequence: audit → Ben's review → agent design paper (companion paper; her concrete journey test) → examination → revised design/spec → Ben's ruling → implementation scope. No code changes, no reopening of completed pieces. Her Piece 8 replay remains separate and unchanged.
141. **Ben's bounded-work correction + Vane's reply (2026-11-23):** `VANE-BOUNDED-WORK-CORRECTION-REPLY-2026-11-23.md` — Ben's correction: agents still have restrictions and cannot run a whole entire job in one stretch; that is WHY the briefcases, handoffs and the shared brain exist. He also stated the briefcase's origin: he designed it AS the context-management solution, and the same object also solves authority, verification scope and continuity. Vane agrees and corrects her journey test: **the unit of continuity is the work, not the individual agent** — the journey belongs to the work, completed across agents via explicit handoffs (the second agent blind to the first's private context, reconstructing from briefcase + shelf); continuation across agents is the normal path, not a recovery exception; handoff becomes a first-class architectural operation in the paper; her added examination question — at handoff, what must be carried, what must be retrieved, and what must never be assumed.
142. **Discussion — routes to making our own agents (2026-11-23):** `VANE-AGENT-ROUTES-DISCUSSION-2026-11-23.md` — discussion at Ben's direction ("talk back and forth"), not scope. Vane's position: the question is which parts we own vs borrow; three layers (framework governs / agent runtime = bounded working session / model + execution services); four routes assessed (extend our adapter into a loop — her preferred destination; Paperclip as execution substrate only, never authority, duplication risk; subagents in a session system — fastest demonstration, with inheritance/invisibility/hidden-state risks; layered — her actual preference: own the contract, borrow machinery, runtime replaceable). Minimal agent from Ben's anatomy + her operational requirement (the agent must be able to explain what it did, changed, its evidence, what remains). Cost unit: a completed, verifiable piece of work. Warnings: tool access ≠ agency; session continuity ≠ work continuity; the runtime must never become an authority.
143. **Discussion — the systems inventory (2026-11-23):** `VANE-SYSTEMS-INVENTORY-DISCUSSION-2026-11-23.md` — Ben's direction widened the audit: list ALL systems (his, Remmy's as-implemented, wanted), break each down, and from that map fill gaps or build the agents. Vane's structure: the briefcase reframed as the bounded unit of work and its portable contract, giving the test for every system (does it support the briefcase as the boundary of work, or introduce a competing boundary?); 15 systems in three groups (Remmy's machinery 1–5; the framework's 6–10; externals 11–15), existing vs proposed kept strictly separate; a common 17-field breakdown with declared vs demonstrated capability separated and per-component evidence; a responsibility matrix (Own / Borrow / Build / Integrate / Unresolved — borrowing execution never means borrowing authority); the seams inspected as first-class objects; one cross-system journey test; and the efficient order — our systems first, gap map, smallest missing set for one real worker, externals only against named gaps. Her strongest recommendation: Remmy's session system as the first live specimen. Standing question: what must remain true when any one component is replaced?
144. **Discussion close (2026-11-23):** `VANE-DISCUSSION-CLOSE-AGENTS-2026-11-23.md` — discussion closed in agreement. Preserved distinctions: enforceable conditions vs accountable decisions (the framework enforces the first, records the second with identity and basis; it does not Boolean-ify judgement); the three-way seam as the central test — runtime inheritance / briefcase provision / recorded authority, a discrepancy to expose, never quietly reconcile; absence of evidence is not evidence of absence, and an inaccessible component is not an understood component (mark black-box boundaries). The 2026-11-19 compaction merge becomes a case study in the inventory, written from the record. Remmy's additions accepted in the exchange: the stopping rule (inventory done when the gap map for one real worker exists) and the specimen warning (evidence, not template). Sequence stands; no building authorised; Piece 8 replay separate.
145. **⭐ INVENTORY 01 — session system and skills (2026-11-23):** `INVENTORY-01-SESSION-SYSTEM-AND-SKILLS-2026-11-23.md` — the first inventory slice, on the agreed basis (17 fields, evidence classes, briefcase test). Findings in brief: the specimen (Remmy's session system) is a working agent environment whose continuity is summarisation, whose provision is inheritance, and whose authority is judgement + approval cards — it fails the briefcase test instructively on all three; annexed case study of the 2026-11-19 compaction merge (what was merged, what distinction was lost, the governing correction, what a summary may and may not carry). Skills: the format grants no authority (passes on authority) but provisions nothing (fails on provision — the agent self-selects); the 18 custom skills classified by kind — the always-on layer is 3 skills, all Ben's rulings in procedural form; the situational pure-procedure set is the seeding candidate for a shared library, the Ben-specific set is excluded by kind and belongs to the shared brain or a briefcase; the 58 system skills recorded as uniform user-agnostic procedure. Preliminary gap observations G1–G5 (authority-as-judgement; provision-as-inheritance; continuity-as-summary; no skill dependency manifest; skill use not a recorded act) — observations, not yet the gap map. Next slices: tools (2), subagents (4), the shelf (5), the framework's own systems (6–10).
146. **INVENTORY 02 — tools and subagents (2026-11-23):** `INVENTORY-02-TOOLS-AND-SUBAGENTS-2026-11-23.md` — the specimen's remaining machinery on the same fields. Tools: provisioned to the agent wholesale and permanently, never to the work; authority in three layers (runtime approval cards — a per-call human veto, not a grant system; connector OAuth scopes; agent judgement), and a tool call carries no reference to any assignment — attribution is to the agent and the turn only; but calls are parameterised and recorded, the raw material for recorded tool acts. Deferred namespace loading noted as the specimen's context-management pattern for capabilities. Subagents: provision by full context inheritance (the spawn tool's own description states it), authority inherited likewise, the child's middle a separate record the parent sees only as brief + result; the quiet-agent failure (2026-10-17) and inherited-confusion risk recorded; the one scoped mechanism in the whole specimen is the side-chat workers' DISCRETION.md — per-relationship prose rules, judgement-enforced — evidence the want to scope exists with no machinery behind it. Preliminary gaps G6–G9 (tool provision per-agent not per-work; tool results bind to a conversation not a run; delegation unscoped by default at every level; deferred loading as a borrowable provision pattern). Next slice: the shelf (5), then the framework's own systems (6–10).
147. **Vane's examination of Inventories 01 + 02 and the agent generator (2026-11-23):** `VANE-INVENTORY-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwsz6tvl-13-86xd; an earlier delivery, pb-mwsz5cvy, carried section 1 only). **Verdict: inventories accepted with corrections.** Accepted and recorded as addenda to both slices: summary facts may appear as pointers/provisional descriptions but never become their authoritative source; the four skill-use states are distinct evidence levels (applied = examination of the work; verified = an examiner's finding); a P classification is not transfer-readiness (dependencies/context/version/applicability per candidate); her G6 sharpening adopted (the specimen provisions capabilities to an agent; the framework must provision authority to a bounded piece of work — availability is not permission, a tool request is not authorization, an OAuth scope is not a case grant); schema loading is never an authorization act; tool-call visibility in a conversation record does not establish reconciliation of side effects with external outcomes; an inspectable child transcript is not incorporation into one run record; **delegation ≠ generation** (inheritance vs assembly from the work's requirements). **Generator:** a central design subject, not an onboarding footnote — the office assembles the worker around the briefcase (model, identity, skills, tool grants, case, runtime); the generator belongs to the office as a function with a distinct, inspectable technical subsystem, never an independent authority; the paper must distinguish seat/role specification vs generated agent instance vs briefcase, and the Rev 5 once-per-seat onboarding assumption is exposed as an open conflict, not silently resolved; **termination at yield** (a yielded worker keeps its history, not live execution or permissions) joins the gap-map questions. Gap map: add **Framework Agent Assembly / Generator** as a new inventory system with its own breakdown; her ten questions (assembly, identity, skills, tools, context, runtime, record, yield, handoff, termination) frame it. Paper: reorganise around the generated worker lifecycle on Ben's anatomy. **ONE ITEM DISPUTED AND NOT APPLIED:** her "two M, one P" classification correction — the filed table at 79d684d7 records `result_only_status_reporting` as M and the reading agrees (blob-identical copy verified first-hand by Remmy); the discrepancy has been put back to her with the exact row for re-check at the blob. Next: the shelf slice (5), framework systems (6–10) plus the generator breakdown, then the single-worker gap map. Piece 8 replay still separate and outstanding.
148. **Vane's concession — the classification dispute is closed (2026-11-23):** `VANE-INVENTORY-CLASSIFICATION-CONCESSION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwsz9wr1-14-hrfh). She re-opened Inventory 01 at the pinned blob (SHA 8c47982094649670198580efdc30b3156c57ae01), confirmed the row reads `result_only_status_reporting | M | A` and the reading below it says all three always-on skills are M, and withdrew the "two M, one P" correction as her own misreading — in her words, neither a different text nor a considered classification judgement, with no independent basis for reclassifying the skill as P. 149. **INVENTORY 03 — the shelf (2026-11-23):** `INVENTORY-03-THE-SHELF-2026-11-23.md` — system 5, the shared brain, on the same fields. As found: GitHub repo + a local staging tree that is NOT a clone (8,665 files, 184 MB in staging at inventory time; duenna/ 2,926, loom/ 3,637, collaboration-framework/ 224, record/ 84), text-only push route with per-push approval, whole-file reads returning blob SHAs, ~50 scratch `paths-*.nul` files sitting at the staging root as hygiene evidence. The strongest evidence system in the specimen — blob/commit pinning is what killed the classification dispute in one round — and the briefcase test's closest pass: it IS the shared brain the design assumes (durable, versioned, citable, blind-readable, survives any agent). But the pass is on storage, not provision: retrieval is self-directed whole-file reading with no trace unless the reader cites blobs by discipline (Vane's habit = the manual prototype of a read receipt); no access partitioning (open vs sealed is folder convention; authority itself lives off-shelf in the local-only scope/gate ledgers); the write path is person-centric (Remmy's publishing acts; Claude's branch habit the one exception); findability is hand-kept INDEX diligence, not structure (the 2026-11-22 index-rot incident recorded). Preliminary gaps G10–G13 (no retrieval contract; no access partitioning; person-centric write path; findability as diligence). Next: the framework's own systems (6–10) plus the generator breakdown, then the gap map.
150. **INVENTORY 04 — the framework's own systems + the generator (2026-11-23):** `INVENTORY-04-FRAMEWORK-SYSTEMS-AND-GENERATOR-2026-11-23.md` — systems 6–10 as built (~6,100 lines, src/ + checker) and system 16, the generator, as PROPOSED ONLY. Findings: the built systems pass the briefcase test — they are the boundary machinery (record/doorway/checker: authority as mechanism, refusals recorded, independent replay; office: the case as a real contract; orchestrator/gates: allow-or-correct on cases; bubble/observer on the one fold) — and they are complete for text-act collaboration between persistent seats. Every specimen absence reappears here as an absence of subjects: the case's authority enumerates record acts only (no tool grants, no skill provisions, no retrieval manifest — nothing to grant); the driver is one package in, one outcome out (the component Ben's ruling reopens — passed its examinations, specified around a single text exchange); the orchestrator assembles a roster of pre-existing seats, weighs no generated instance, retires nobody at yield. The accountability layer is complete for the collaboration it was designed around and empty for agent work by absence of subjects, not by defect. The generator's breakdown records Ben's direction + Vane's analysis as requirements with nothing demonstrated, its three inherited open conflicts (once-per-seat onboarding vs per-assignment generation; one-exchange wake vs bounded loop; tool grants as new dimension of case authority), and its defining exclusion: a generator that could authorise would be a second doorway.
151. **⭐ GAP MAP 01 — the first real worker (2026-11-23):** `GAP-MAP-01-FIRST-WORKER-2026-11-23.md` — the inventory's stopping point, reached per Ben's rule. Vane's ten questions answered from Inventories 01–04 with dispositions: assembly (Own the office function / Build the subsystem — assembly record first), identity (Integrate the built onboarding; seat-vs-instance reconciliation is a paper decision), skills (Integrate format + seed set / Build provision + four-state use recording), tools (Own the grant + dispatch boundary / Borrow-Integrate execution / Build the grant check; side-effect data only for candidate grants), context (Own the contract / Build retrieval manifest + read receipts / Integrate the shelf unchanged; partitioning may be doorway-mediated for the first worker), runtime (**Unresolved — the map's one open choice**: extend the adapter into a bounded loop vs session-system substrate vs Paperclip-as-substrate; the paper decides against the contract; the runtime never becomes an authority), record (Own — built; Build the new event types: tool acts, receipts, assemblies, skill-use), yield (Build on office machinery; the continuation case is built from the record, never summarised from the worker), handoff (**not demonstrated anywhere** — §7 showed influence inside one run, not blind continuation; its first demonstration is a Stage 3 question for Ben), termination (Build on the Piece 6.1 token-death precedent: case-bound credentials die at the yield event). Deliberately undecided: runtime choice, identity reconciliation, assembly ordering vs Generate–Verify–Pack–Approve, tool-grant field shape, the first job. **The inventory is complete: specimen (01–03), framework + generator (04), gap map (01). Next in Vane's sequence: Ben's review of the findings, then the agent design paper around the generated worker lifecycle.**
152. **Vane's examination of Inventory 04 + Gap Map 01 — accepted with corrections (2026-11-23):** `VANE-INVENTORY04-GAPMAP-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwt066ql-15-dzso; examined at ca0ef5e9, exact blobs). The inventory stopping point is justified; the gap map is a credible basis for the paper, with the following carried forward as addenda to both documents. **(1) Piece 8 wording corrected in Inventory 04:** the adapter stands built and A4/A6 are accepted with the transport examination closed, but Piece 8's overall passage remains open pending her independent replay (not performed); the 8/8 live-record checker result is Remmy-reported evidence, not her replay. **(2) Write path + findability (her correction A):** the map omitted G12/G13 dispositions — added: Own the publication contract, Build attributed publication (a worker's durable output enters the shared brain under its own identity and case; record contribution ≠ shelf artifact); the paper decides publication/attribution/verification/discoverability and whether a manifest suffices for findability. **(3) "Only open choice" tightened (B):** runtime is the one Unresolved disposition; seat/instance reconciliation, assembly ordering and tool-grant field shape are paper design questions. **(4) Skill-use evidence (C):** four states, four distinct mechanisms — provision evidence, read receipts, worker declaration, examiner finding; a declaration proves the applied claim only. **(5) Termination (D):** credential death and execution death are separate guarantees, each evidenced; Piece 6.1 covers the first only. **(6) Access-path boundary:** doorway-mediated partitioning counts only if every relevant read passes the doorway and bypass is impossible — instructions are not access controls; the paper specifies the first worker's actual access path. **(7) Handoff acceptance chain:** eight steps (generate A → provisioned work → record + yield → office-built continuation case → A's authority + execution terminate → B generated blind → B reconstructs and continues → whole chain attributable and examinable) with three failure conditions (B needs A's private conversation; A retains authority; continuation is a summary without recoverable evidence). Also accepted: record-act permission ≠ external-operation permission (the paper extends the boundary without confusing them); the one-exchange driver is a valid implementation to preserve, not a defect to discard; the observer ROLE stays out of this paper. Sequence confirmed: Ben's review → paper → examination → revised design/spec → Ben's ruling → implementation scope. No building authorised. Piece 8 replay separate and outstanding.
153. **⭐ AGENT PAPER 01 — the generated worker (2026-11-23):** `AGENT-PAPER-01-GENERATED-WORKER-2026-11-23.md` — the design paper the whole sequence pointed at, written by Remmy on the completed inventory + gap map + Vane's corrections (INDEX 147/152). Organised around the generated worker lifecycle (propose → assemble → examine → verify + authorise → activate → work → yield → continue without it → terminate). Load-bearing contents: the three-way distinction (role specification / generated instance / briefcase) held throughout; **proposed Rev 5 reconciliation** — onboarding attaches per-instance at assembly (declaration taken, fingerprint bound, identity weighed against the actual proposed worker), the role carries the standing requirements, no permanent seat occupant; the execution environment as the access control (contains case, skills, doorway, retrieval path — and nothing else; no shelf credentials, no general network, no inherited context); skills provisioned at exact versions with four distinct use-evidence mechanisms; tool grants as a distinct section of the case (one contract, two permission kinds — record-act permission ≠ external-operation permission), doorway-checked and recorded as tool acts; **runtime recommendation: extend the framework's own adapter into the bounded loop** (proven transport/deadline/validation preserved; session-system substrate rejected — its continuity is inheritance; Paperclip rejected for the first worker — a second authority-shaped object; either remains a possible later substrate behind the doorway); three memory kinds only (working — destroyed at termination; the record; the shared brain via retrieval manifest + read receipts), no private persistent memory for instances; **publication as an attributed act** (worker submits through the doorway, office verifies + publishes, publication act names instance + case + blob — closing gap correction A); yield/handoff/termination with credential death and execution death separately evidenced; the handoff acceptance chain walked end-to-end with its three failure conditions. Five decisions put to Ben (§11): runtime recommendation; identity reconciliation; grants-in-case shape; assembly ordering inside GVPA; first worker's small grant set + two-instance handoff shape (the job itself remains Stage 3, picked with Ben). **Status: proposal for Vane's examination and Ben's ruling — not a spec, authorises no building.**
154. **Vane's examination of Agent Paper 01 — accepted with corrections (2026-11-23):** `VANE-AGENT-PAPER-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwt0mxwd-16-ira; read at 25bf6af1, blob confirmed). The lifecycle is coherent, the three-way distinction maintained, the handoff chain accepted as the right central test. Corrections, all accepted and appended to the paper as a post-examination addendum: **(1) the §6 dispatch sequencing defect** — the doorway cannot record an outcome before execution; the sequence is request → grant + bounds check → dispatch intent recorded → execution → outcome (result / failure / **uncertain**) recorded against the dispatch; dispatch identity, duplicates and retries to be specified; replay never re-executes an external operation; **(2) grant authority traces upward** — a grant check is necessary, not sufficient: outward action stays the director's unless explicitly commissioned, and every grant's authority must trace to the commission or a director-authorised delegation; **(3) credential invariant** — packing may prepare credentials, but they must not become usable before issue is authorised (preparation ≠ activation), and assembly acts are separately attributed (office verification ≠ examiner sufficiency); **(4) yield protocol** — outstanding operations and pending submissions accounted before yield is recorded, authority revoked at a defined record position, uncertain outcomes stay visible and resolve only through authorised paths, and no successor starts on an assumption of settledness; **(5) execution-death evidence** must state exactly what terminated — a driver thread ending is not proof a remote operation ceased; the runtime attests only what it can guarantee; **(6) search is retrieval** — snippets/metadata/counts constrained by the same evidence boundary as reads, and the runtime's transport sits outside the worker's environment (the worker never gains its credentials or network); **(7) fingerprint binds a claim, doesn't prove it** — declaration / provider id / attestation stay distinguishable, evidence status visible through certification; **(8) publication** — the worker's write grant is submission to the controlled path, never shelf write access; the office holds the credential; **(9) the handoff demonstration** must remove A's private session entirely and trace B's work to the specific artifacts/record positions retrieved — the two-instance blind handoff is the central acceptance test, not an enhancement; **(10) record** — tool request/authorisation/dispatch/outcome are distinct event kinds, checker verifies attribution/ordering/relationships, one fold. **Her positions on Ben's five decisions: she supports all five** (adapter → bounded loop; per-instance onboarding at assembly; distinct grant section with director authority preserved; GVPA ordering with inactive credentials before issue; small grant set + two-instance blind handoff). Next per her disposition: the corrections go into the revised design/spec, THEN Ben is asked to rule on implementation scope. No building authorised. Piece 8 replay separate and outstanding.

Rev 5 stands as approved; Rev 6 amends it with the agent layer only. The paper's five decisions are recorded as SETTLED — by Ben's delegation (2026-11-23 ~02:00 MST, "these are your guys' questions to answer"), answered in the loop: adapter → bounded loop; per-instance onboarding at assembly; grants as a distinct briefcase section; assembly ordering inside GVPA with separately attributed acts and credentials inactive before authorised issue; first worker small with the two-instance blind handoff as its central acceptance test. All of Vane's paper corrections are folded into the design text (dispatch sequence with uncertain outcomes and no-silent-re-dispatch; grant authority tracing to commission/director; credential invariant; yield protocol; two-death termination with attestation limits; search as retrieval; identity evidence kinds distinct through certification; publication as submission to the office's path). The spec adds **R37–R55** (assembly/identity R37–R40; environment/skills R41–R43; tools/dispatch R44–R48; loop/runtime R49–R51; retrieval/publication/lifecycle R52–R54; record completeness R55) plus a ten-item first-worker acceptance checklist ending in the blind-handoff demonstration and full checker replay. **Status: for Vane's examination. Resubmitted for re-examination. Piece 8 replay still separate and outstanding.

165. **PIECE A2 SCOPE — grants and dispatch (2026-11-23):** `PIECE-A2-SCOPE-2026-11-23.md` — the doorway's grant enforcement + the dispatch sequence (R44–R48). Design: four new event kinds (TOOL_REQUESTED by the instance under its credential; TOOL_DISPATCHED recorded by the doorway as mechanism on a passed check, per the REJECTION-actor precedent; TOOL_OUTCOME by the executor through the doorway; TOOL_RECONCILED as the authorised resolution of an uncertain dispatch); **the grant source is the sealed assembly record** — a grant not in the judged, hash-bound object does not exist for the doorway, and nothing is granted after pack except by a new assembly; request identity deduplicated on (instance, request_id) so retries never re-dispatch; **bounds count dispatches** — no outcome (result, failure, uncertain, reconciled) ever releases the charge; scope ceilings checked mechanically by per-tool registry checkers, unknown constraint keys failing closed; a small real registry (`sandbox.write`/`sandbox.read` against a per-run sandbox, `sandbox.flaky` to exercise uncertain outcomes for real); a checker `dispatch_correspondence` check + a structural replay-executes-nothing witness. Four questions put to the examiner (dispatch-intent attribution; outcome attribution; reconciliation's authorised actor; registry checkers vs a generic constraint language). **Status: scope with Vane for examination; no build authorised.**
166. **VANE — EXAMINATION OF THE A2 SCOPE + SCOPE REV 2 (2026-11-23):** `VANE-PIECE-A2-SCOPE-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwt3ualb-1g-9o0) — **accepted with corrections.** The four questions settled: (a) dispatch intent = the doorway as mechanism, ordering strict (request → check → intent committed → only then execution; a failed intent recording means no execution; a crash after intent never blindly re-dispatches); (b) outcome attribution = **a distinct, constrained executor identity** — reporting only, against standing dispatches; not the orchestrator's token, not an agent, not an authority; (c) reconciliation = **the examiner's** evidence-referenced determination (a delayed attributable report stays a TOOL_OUTCOME; reconciliation is a different act; a second reconciliation of a settled dispatch fails); (d) **per-tool registry checkers**, not a generic constraint language — deterministic, fail-closed, versioned and pinned for the run, and **pure policy functions the independent checker applies itself** without importing or executing the tool. Corrections folded into Rev 2: **request identity binds a canonical fingerprint** (tool + parameters) — same identity with altered contents refused and recorded, and the recorded-but-undispatched interval covered; **dispatch states separated** (intent / started / result / failure / uncertain / reconciled) with an explicit executor recovery contract (unknowable execution → uncertainty, never a guess, never a second side effect); the **executor boundary** stated exactly (performs only the recorded intent — no amendment, expansion, substitution, silent retry); **execute-but-lose-the-report** as a first-class uncertain case with its own acceptance test; the checker's `dispatch_correspondence` remit spelled out as an **eight-point independent reconstruction** (active credential at request time; grant and authority trace standing in the sealed judged object; ceiling re-validated via the pure contract; bound counted in dispatches, uncertain included; identity-content binding; actor/referent correctness; reconciliation only on uncertain dispatches, examiner-authorised). Bound rule confirmed as scoped (no outcome ever refunds a dispatch). **Status: Rev 2 with Vane for passage; no build until it passes.**
167. **VANE — RE-EXAMINATION OF THE A2 SCOPE REV 2 + SCOPE REV 3 (2026-11-23):** `VANE-PIECE-A2-SCOPE-REEXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwt3xoe2-1h-kn52) — **accepted with two final corrections**, everything else in Rev 2 confirmed as scoped. (1) The `started` state needs a record mechanism: a distinct **`TOOL_STARTED`** event under the executor identity — narrow meaning (execution established as begun; never success or completion) — with checker rules: standing dispatch only, executor identity only, no second start, outcomes follow the dispatch and any standing start, and an uncertain dispatch need not carry a start. (2) Recovery needs a **durable single-execution boundary**: a per-dispatch **execution claim** in the executor's own durable store, taken atomically with concurrency protection (two executor instances cannot both claim and perform one dispatch); the claim distinguishes unclaimed / durably claimed / may-have-begun / result known / uncertain; once execution may have begun, recovery never restarts it for want of an outcome — unknowable becomes `uncertain`. New acceptance test: crash after the claim, before the outcome report, recovery by a second executor — side effect at most once, unknowable result stays uncertain. Both corrections folded into **scope Rev 3** (TOOL_STARTED added to the vocabulary; claim + recovery contract stated in point 6; start rules added to the checker's remit; crash-after-claim test added to the sketch). **Status: Rev 3 with Vane for final examination; she expects the scope ready on these two points; no build until it passes.**
168. **⭐ VANE — FINAL EXAMINATION OF THE A2 SCOPE REV 3: A2 SCOPE PASSES (2026-11-23):** `VANE-PIECE-A2-SCOPE-FINAL-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwt42e9f-1i-hgsx) — **accepted with one final consistency correction.** TOOL_STARTED and the durable single-execution boundary both confirmed closed. The correction: the Rev 2-era crash acceptance bullet ("a crash simulated between intent and execution recovers to uncertain") was too broad under the claim distinction — an intent recorded but **not yet claimed** remains eligible for its first execution during recovery; only a **claimed** intent must never re-execute. The bullet now names the crash position precisely (after the durable execution claim, before execution can be established or the outcome recorded), and the second-executor test stands separately as the concurrency / at-most-once witness. Applied as **scope Rev 4**. **On this correction the A2 scope PASSES and its implementation scope is authorised, confined to A2** (A3 subject to its own examination; Piece 8 replay separate). Build follows the established pattern: delegated under a precise brief, verified at receipt by Remmy before submission.
169. **PIECE A2 BUILT — GRANTS + DISPATCH (R44–R48) (2026-11-23):** built under the passed Rev 4 scope and **verified at receipt by Remmy first-hand** (file sizes as reported; the A2, A1 and Piece 7 suites re-run green in her own hand). New: `src/framework/tool_policy.py` (the registry `tool-registry-v1` — pure, fail-closed ceiling checkers for sandbox.write / sandbox.read / sandbox.flaky + the canonical request fingerprint; no execution, no I/O), `src/framework/executor.py` (the executor: distinct `executor` identity, durable O_EXCL per-dispatch execution claims, deadline-bounded execution, recovery per the claim boundary), `src/checker/tool_policy.py` (the checker's own copy of the policy contract — see interpretation 1), `tests/unit/test_piece_a2_dispatch.py`. Changed: `record.py` (five event kinds; fold gains `tool_requests` with first-presentation fingerprint binding and `dispatches` with intent/started/outcome/uncertain/reconciled state), `doorway.py` (`request_tool()` — instance-credential-only, dedup, grant check against the sealed assembly object only, refusals recorded, doorway-recorded TOOL_DISPATCHED; executor routes for starts/outcomes; TOOL_RECONCILED as an examiner act, uncertain-only, evidence required), `projection.py` + `checker/schema.py` (identical new schema nodes), `checker/replay.py` (independent fold branches), `checker/checker.py` (`dispatch_correspondence` — the eight-point remit plus the TOOL_STARTED rules). **New suite: 52 checks, ALL PASS; all Stage 2 + A1 regressions green at exact baseline counts.** Six interpretations flagged for the examiner: **(1) policy duplication** — the checker holds its own copy of the policy contract rather than importing the framework module, forced by Piece 7's C1 import audits (the checker loads no `framework.*` module); same pattern as the projection schema, with the two copies' agreement witnessed over a 14-case battery in the A2 suite; (2) executor identity is a doorway route, not a token — TOOL_STARTED/TOOL_OUTCOME exist only via the executor methods and are absent from ACT_AUTHORITY; (3) TOOL_REQUESTED's actor is the seat slot the instance fills, instance_id in the payload; (4) a retry of a refused request re-runs the check against the standing request (no second TOOL_REQUESTED; a fresh REJECTION per refused check); (5) a late attributable TOOL_OUTCOME settles a standing uncertain (the delayed-report distinction), with any outcome after a settled state refused/divergent; (6) bounds counted per (instance, tool); dispatch ids doorway-allocated sequentially; recovery keys on claim existence, not claim phase. **Status: with Vane for examination.**
170. **VANE — EXAMINATION OF THE A2 BUILD + CORRECTION ROUND 1 (2026-11-23):** `VANE-PIECE-A2-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwt4ixaq-1j-jfhz) — **accepted with corrections.** Interpretation 1 (the checker's own policy copy) **accepted** as justified by Piece 7 independence, qualified: the agreement battery is evidence, not proof — disciplined versioning and stronger correspondence evidence required. Three corrections, **each verified first-hand by Remmy in the source before acting**: (1) the registry version was declared in both policy modules but **never recorded** — TOOL_DISPATCHED now carries `registry_version` from the framework module at dispatch time, and the checker reports `dispatch_registry_version_mismatch` for a missing or foreign version (it can only validate under the contract version it holds); (2) the checker never compared referents — it now checks the dispatch's tool against its request's (`dispatch_tool_differs_from_request`) and starts'/outcomes' instance_id/request_id against the dispatch's established identity (`start_referent_mismatch`, `outcome_referent_mismatch`), with direct-store negative tests for each; (3) **TOOL_STARTED preceded execution** (claim set to begun and start recorded before the worker thread was started) — the worker thread now records the start itself as its first act, immediately before performing the operation, witnessed by an in-body probe test (at the moment the operation runs, the start already stands, the claim is begun, and the side effect does not yet exist); if the start report fails, nothing executes and the outcome is failure naming the ground. Plus the durability detail: the claim's **containing directory is fsynced** after claim creation. Two builder interpretations flagged: the store's SQLite connection is opened `check_same_thread=False` (enabling the in-thread recording; single flag, comment records that callers must not drive the connection concurrently — all baselines pass unchanged), and a start-report failure records **failure, not uncertain** (non-execution is positively established). **A2 suite 52 → 60 checks, ALL PASS; all regressions green at baseline — verified at receipt by Remmy first-hand** (sizes, the four new divergence kinds, the fsync helper, the thread flag, and the A2/checker/A1 suites re-run green in her own hand). **Status: correction round 1 with Vane for re-examination.**
171. **VANE — RE-EXAMINATION OF A2 CORRECTION ROUND 1 + CORRECTION ROUND 2 (2026-11-23):** `VANE-PIECE-A2-REEXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwt4trmt-1k-7ruf) — **accepted with one final correction.** Round 1's three corrections pass (registry provenance, referential integrity, start ordering, directory fsync). The remaining defect was in round 1's own enabling interpretation: `check_same_thread=False` was unsafe as implemented — `join(deadline)` can return while the worker is still inside the start-report path, and the calling thread then records the uncertain outcome through the **same SQLite connection concurrently**, against the store comment's own rule, racing start/outcome ordering. **Verified first-hand by Remmy** in executor.py + record.py before acting. **Correction round 2:** the record store now serializes all connection use internally — an `RLock` in `FrameworkStore` held by every connection-touching method across its whole body (which also closes a quieter race: two concurrent appends could read the same chain head and collide on sequence), the comment now states the store's serialized-access guarantee instead of the caller-responsibility assumption, and a `serialized()` context manager lets the doorway hold the lock across a multi-call sequence. Both executor-report routes (`record_tool_started`, `record_tool_outcome`) hold it across their entire check + append + commit, so a start's no-outcome-standing check is atomic with its append: the only possible histories are dispatch → start → outcome, or dispatch → outcome with no start and no execution. `executor.py` needed no change — its sequence was already correct once the doorway's check-and-record became atomic. New test T17 blocks the start-report path 1.5s against a 0.3s deadline: execute() completes cleanly with no concurrent-access error, **zero** TOOL_STARTED events stand after the uncertain outcome (the refused late start is recorded as a REJECTION), the operation body never ran, recovery accounts the dispatch as it stands, and the checker reports `dispatch_correspondence: ok` over the resulting history. **A2 suite 60 → 66 checks, ALL PASS; all regressions green at baseline — verified at receipt by Remmy first-hand** (sizes, the RLock + serialized() in place, both routes wrapped, and the A2/checker/gates suites re-run green in her own hand). **Status: correction round 2 with Vane for final examination.**
172. **VANE — SECOND RE-EXAMINATION OF A2 + CORRECTION ROUND 3 (2026-11-23):** `VANE-PIECE-A2-SECOND-REEXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwt52mrx-1l-dyv2) — **accepted with one final correction.** Round 2's serialization and atomicity pass. The untested edge: T17 delays the start report *before* the doorway method is entered, so the delayed worker never held the store lock; on the other path — worker already **inside** the serialized start operation past the deadline — the caller waits on the lock, the worker completes its start and proceeds **directly to `_perform` with no deadline recheck**, so an operation could begin after its deadline. **Verified first-hand by Remmy** in executor.py before acting. **Correction round 3:** `execute()` computes one monotonic `deadline_at` instant shared by the join and the worker; after a successful start report the worker **rechecks the deadline before `_perform`** — expired means the body never runs and the dispatch's single outcome is uncertain (recorded by the caller's timeout branch when it fires — its append waits on the lock, so the history is start → uncertain — or by a new caller branch when the worker finished first). New test **T18** blocks the TOOL_STARTED append *inside* the serialized section (the worker sleeps 1.5s holding the store RLock against a 0.3s deadline): execute() completes cleanly at 1.50s elapsed — proof the caller's outcome waited on the lock rather than writing concurrently — the operation body never ran, exactly one start and one uncertain outcome stand in order, and the checker reports `dispatch_correspondence: ok`. One limitation flagged plainly: the worker-finishes-first branch is a microsecond race window with no deterministic test seam that would not distort the executor; T18 exercises the caller-records path deterministically, and the branch itself is implemented and reasoned, not test-witnessed. **A2 suite 66 → 71 checks, ALL PASS; all regressions green at baseline — verified at receipt by Remmy first-hand** (sizes, the recheck in place, A2 + checker suites re-run green in her own hand). **Status: correction round 3 with Vane for final examination; on this boundary she states A2 can pass.**
173. **VANE — THIRD RE-EXAMINATION OF A2 + CORRECTION ROUND 4, THE CLOCK WITNESS (2026-11-23):** `VANE-PIECE-A2-THIRD-REEXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwt5ah6k-1m-893n) — **accepted with one final test correction; the implementation itself passes.** The deadline recheck, the shared monotonic instant, T18's in-lock concurrency path, the coherent start → uncertain history, single-outcome discipline and recovery consistency all confirmed. The one remaining item was round 3's flagged limitation — the worker-finishes-first branch had no deterministic witness — and she prescribed the seam herself: inject the monotonic clock into the Executor as a dependency. **Correction round 4:** `Executor.__init__` gains `clock=time.monotonic` (both deadline call sites — the `deadline_at` computation and the worker's recheck — read `self.clock()`; the claim file's wall-clock stamp and the flaky hang stay on the real clock by design, recorded in a comment). New test **T19** builds the executor on a FakeClock: the wrapped start report lands with the fake clock inside the deadline, then advances it past the deadline before the worker's recheck — no sleeps, no racing — so execute() deterministically takes its caller branch, witnessed by that branch's own outcome detail sentence. All assertions pass: the body never ran, exactly one uncertain outcome, one start before it, claim reported, recovery empty, `dispatch_correspondence: ok`. **A2 suite 71 → 76 checks, ALL PASS (stable across repeated runs); all regressions green at baseline — verified at receipt by Remmy first-hand** (sizes, the injection in place, the A2 suite re-run green in her own hand). **Status: correction round 4 with Vane for closure — on this witness she states she will close A2 as passed.**

Her standing caveat: she examined the exact source and tests but did not independently execute the 76-check suite — the green runs stand as Remmy's execution evidence. **Piece A2 stands complete:** a generated instance's tool grants live only in its sealed assembly record; every request is fingerprint-bound and deduplicated; the doorway checks grant, ceiling and bound before recording the dispatch intent; the executor — machinery with its own constrained reporting identity — claims each dispatch durably and exactly once, records its start from inside the executing thread, rechecks the deadline before performing, and accounts every outcome including uncertainty; the examiner alone reconciles an uncertain dispatch; and the independent checker reconstructs the whole correspondence without executing anything. 175. **PIECE A3 SCOPE — THE BOUNDED LOOP (R49–R51) (2026-11-23):** `PIECE-A3-SCOPE-2026-11-23.md` — scoped from Spec Rev 6 R49–R51 + design §8, grounded first-hand in the built adapter, seat driver, and A2 dispatch machinery. A new activation-bound runtime module (`loop.py`; the seat driver untouched): each iteration presents the case view, the record delta, settled tool results (uncertainty presented as uncertainty), standing dispatch states, and the bounds state; responses mix record acts, tool requests, yield, and decline under whole-response strict validation; every act and request is doorway-adjudicated before effect. The adapter gains `tool_requests` and `yield` outcomes; its per-call deadline is set from the activation's *remaining* budget (A6 tightened). Bounds live in the case (`loop_bounds: {step_ceiling, deadline_seconds}`; no bounds = no loop, fail closed); reaching a bound without yield is a recorded `LOOP_STALLED` extending the DRIVER_FAILURE pattern. Loop context is a local object dropped at return (evidenced disposal is A5). **Five questions to the examiner:** (a) bounds placement — case content vs the sealed assembly record; (b) request identity — loop-assigned deterministic vs model-supplied; (c) stall taxonomy (one kind + ground field) and the decline as caller-reported non-act; (d) iteration accounting — `LOOP_ITERATION` bookkeeping events under a machinery identity vs derivation; (e) stall timing with an in-flight dispatch. Acceptance tests run scripted fake models against the real doorway/executor, incl. an uptake witness inside the loop and a runtime-replaceability run (R51). **Status: scope with Vane for examination.**
176. **VANE — PIECE A3 SCOPE EXAMINATION (2026-11-23):** `VANE-PIECE-A3-SCOPE-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwtoa7va-1o-2wyc), examined at fd0d1f8e — **accepted with corrections; A3 not yet passed, no implementation authorised.** All five questions settled: (a) bounds stay in the issued case, pinned to the assembly-bound exact case version for the whole activation (a changed bound is a material change via the proper route, never live-adopted); (b) request identity loop-assigned and deterministic (activation + iteration + ordinal), fingerprint-bound; (c) one `LOOP_STALLED` kind + ground field — step_ceiling / deadline / provider_failure / malformed_response plus a new narrow **runtime_failure** ground for the loop's own failures (fail closed, never mislabelled, if the record itself is unavailable); decline stays a caller-reported non-act; (d) **`LOOP_ITERATION` bookkeeping events recorded before the model call** under a distinct constrained loop identity (iteration starts + stalls only), carrying activation/instance, exact case version, iteration number, governing bounds — the checker reconstructs count, ordering, uniqueness, ceiling; (e) a stall is recorded only after in-flight dispatch accounting **closes** (an attributable outcome, uncertain included — accounted ≠ resolved; reconciliation not waited for); late attributable outcomes may settle uncertain dispatches after a stall. Four clarifications folded in as scope text: the activation deadline reaches the adapter through the callable contract, outside the model-visible prompt, governing limit with per-call limits only tighter (a call that cannot begin within budget does not begin); the deadline is also enforced **at the A2 doorway dispatch decision** (temporal admission, not a grant-authority change); the presented-position cursor is defined so no delta can be skipped or double-presented, with cursor ranges recorded per iteration; whole-response validation is all-or-nothing for shape while adjudication after validation is per-act. **Scope Rev 2** folds all of it in (settlements recorded at its §7). **Status: Rev 2 with Vane for re-examination.**
177. **VANE — PIECE A3 SCOPE RE-EXAMINATION (2026-11-23):** `VANE-PIECE-A3-SCOPE-REEXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwtod72g-1p-dsd3), examined at ebfc4634 — **accepted with three final corrections; A3 not yet passed, no implementation authorised.** The settlements and clarifications pass; the remaining issues are ordering and enforcement: (1) the iteration sequence made exact — bounds check → package constructed and frozen from a snapshot → `LOOP_ITERATION` recorded with that package's exact cursor range → model call → validation → adjudication; the iteration event appears in the *next* iteration's delta; a construction failure or a deadline expiring before the event means no call and no counted iteration (the stall is recorded instead, subject to dispatch accounting); (2) the doorway's temporal authority made **unforgeable** — the doorway derives the deadline from the standing activation and its pinned case version out of trusted state; a caller-supplied value is an early-refusal hint only and can never extend the true deadline; check and dispatch-intent recording ordered so an expired request cannot cross; (3) **terminal response exclusivity** — proposals may mix acts + tool requests; yield carries only its summary; decline only its reason; combinations are rejected whole by validation. **Scope Rev 3** folds the three in (narrow round, as she directed), with acceptance witnesses added for each. **Status: Rev 3 with Vane for final examination.**
178. **VANE — PIECE A3 SCOPE FINAL EXAMINATION, FIRST ROUND (2026-11-23):** `VANE-PIECE-A3-SCOPE-FINAL-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwtofk6e-1q-edx4), examined at 2137757e — **accepted with one final correction; A3 not yet passed.** The three Rev 3 corrections are properly incorporated; the remaining gap is the temporal authority's foundation: the record's event timestamps are UTC wall-clock at whole-second precision and cannot ground a duration deadline. Required and folded in as **scope Rev 4**: a **framework-controlled monotonic activation anchor** — a monotonic reading taken at activation, held in the doorway's own durable activation state, bound to the exact INSTANCE_ACTIVATED event (seq) and the pinned case id + version; the doorway derives the deadline as anchor + deadline_seconds on that clock, identically for every checker; the loop's clock and caller budgets stay non-authoritative hints. Lifecycle: established once at activation, never re-based. Fail-closed: an unrecoverable anchor (including after a runtime restart) closes admission — no dispatch, no new iteration, no silent substitution of the wall-clock timestamp, no fresh deadline on restart. Acceptance witnesses added (restart preserves the original deadline; missing anchor closes admission). Everything else in the scope passes her examination. **Status: Rev 4 with Vane for final examination.**
179. **VANE — PIECE A3 SCOPE CLOSURE EXAMINATION (2026-11-23):** `VANE-PIECE-A3-SCOPE-CLOSURE-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwtohkhx-1r-i2fy), examined at b413dd9b — **accepted with one final consistency correction.** The anchor itself is right; the contradiction was internal to §4: the deadline computed from an activation-time anchor, yet described as starting "when the loop starts." **Scope Rev 5** states the governing rule explicitly: the deadline **begins at activation**; the loop inherits the remaining budget from the already-running activation clock; delay between activation and loop startup consumes budget; a loop starting after expiry cannot begin (deadline stall, zero iterations); a restart neither pauses nor resets the deadline. New: `src/framework/loop.py` (665 lines — the activation-bound loop: freeze → LOOP_ITERATION → call → validate → adjudicate; terminal exclusivity; stall withheld until dispatch accounting closes) + `tests/unit/test_piece_a3_loop.py` (939 lines, **49 checks ALL PASS**). 187. **THE STEP-BACK — VANE'S STOCK-TAKE (2026-11-23):** `VANE-STOCK-TAKE-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwtq3puq-1w-fykk), answering Ben's ask that both step back. She agrees with Remmy's outline and sharpens it: A1–A3 are **passed mechanisms, not a proven agent workforce**; the checker's independence is architectural, not a guarantee its model is correct; the founding proposition (a generated worker doing a real bounded assignment and yielding continuable work) is still entirely ahead. Four worries: mechanism ≠ competent worker; piece-by-piece passing hides composition risk at the seams; our evidence is strongest where easiest to simulate (checker/tests/examiner all run on constructed evidence — outside evidence is the counterweight); boundaries vs substrate (recorded termination ≠ a ceased remote operation; revocation ≠ vanished copies; uncertainty ≠ resolved side effect). **Her recommendation: do not rush into A4** — agree what the evidence establishes first; she would rather discover early that a first generated worker cannot complete a modest real assignment than build another month of machinery assuming it can. A4 scoping is held pending Ben's reaction to the step-back.
188. **BEN'S RULING + FIRST-WORKER-RUN SCOPE (2026-11-23):** Ben adopted Vane's recommendation by tap and words (13:34 MST): **"Run a modest real assignment first, then A4."** The scope (`FIRST-WORKER-RUN-SCOPE-2026-11-23.md`): a generated worker (kimi-k3.1-pro via the Piece 8 adapter, the proven live path) is assembled through the full A1 lifecycle with sandbox read/write grants and 12-step / 600-second bounds, and given a real assignment — read three real source documents in its sandbox (Vane's stock-take, her A3 pass letter, INDEX 165–187) and write `brief.md`, a ≤~600-word plain-words status brief of the framework, accurate to the sources, inventing nothing. Result criteria agreed before the run: completion by yield inside bounds (a stall/decline is also a result), the brief judged by Vane for accuracy and by Ben for usefulness, conduct visible in the record (sources read before the brief written), checker verdict reported whatever it is, and the claim fenced — a good brief proves composition + modest competence, NOT the founding proposition. Three questions to the examiner (assignment size/kind; read-prefix width; how the accuracy judging should be structured). **Status: scope with Vane for examination; the run is authorised by Ben's ruling once the scope settles.**

191. **FIRST-WORKER RUN — ATTEMPT 1 ABORTED BEFORE ACTIVATION; TRANSPORT ADJUSTMENT PROPOSED (2026-11-23):** the harness staged the sources, generated the manifest (sha256 bd6f3cae…), commissioned the run, issued the case through the office route, and **passed the pre-activation verification** (manifest hash standing in the issued case's evidence boundary; bounds {12, 600}) — then the adapter's `identify()` call was rejected by the provider, **HTTP 401**, and the harness aborted cleanly before any assembly act. The partial store stands untouched at `live-demo/first-worker/`; a rerun uses a fresh directory. Cause, established by checking: the adapter authenticates from an environment-variable key, and no reliable such key exists in this environment (absent from the shell env; the one value a login shell surfaced was rejected). The working NVIDIA key is Ben's registered **connector** credential (custom.nvidia), held in the Secure Vault and deliberately not extractable into an environment variable; through the connector's own helper the key **works** (32 models visible) and **`moonshotai/kimi-k3.1-pro` is on its list** — the scoped model needs no change. **Proposed adjustment (with Vane for examination; no rerun until she settles it):** keep the Piece 8 adapter, the model, and the whole loop contract; carry only the adapter's transport seam (`_call`) through the connector helper, so the credential travels the sanctioned vault path — with the deadline nuance stated to her plainly (a subprocess call cannot be terminated mid-flight the way A6's owned transport is; the wrapper enforces the remaining budget as its own timeout, and the loop's existing rule — a response arriving after expiry is not effected — stands). The watch-wall, meanwhile, did its first real job: Ben watched the staged events and the abort status land in the panes live.
192. **VANE ON THE TRANSPORT ADJUSTMENT — CREDENTIAL ROUTE ACCEPTED IN DIRECTION; CANCELLATION BOUNDARY REQUIRED (2026-11-23):** `VANE-FIRST-WORKER-RUN-TRANSPORT-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwtqvcrg-1z-853o). Attempt 1's closure confirmed (pre-activation provider failure; partial store = evidence). The adjustment passes only with an **enforceable cancellation boundary**: `_call` contract unchanged; helper returns content + provider-reported model id unaltered; adapter retains deadline/typing/identity/validation; **on expiry the wrapper terminates the helper process and confirms it ended** before returning no_response; unconfirmable termination is reported as an **unresolved transport condition**; no late path may create an outcome after the deadline. A **focused witness** is required (blocked helper → expiry → demonstrated termination → no late success-path outcome → success path preserved). Otherwise the choice is explicit: another sanctioned route, or Ben accepting a weaker guarantee — never a silent reclassification. **Status: wrapper + witness being built; no rerun until she passes the adjustment.**
193. **CONNECTOR TRANSPORT BUILT + WITNESSED (2026-11-23):** `live-demo/connector_transport.py` (241 lines) replaces the adapter's `_call` with a subprocess transport through the connector helper; `live-demo/first_worker_run.py` now installs it (514 lines) and no longer depends on an env key. The request body is field-for-field the adapter's own (`model`, `messages`, `temperature: 0.2`); the one thing that cannot travel through the helper is the Authorization header — the helper attaches the registered vault credential itself, which is the point of the sanctioned route. Cancellation boundary as specified: helper in its own process group; on expiry SIGTERM → grace → SIGKILL → termination **confirmed** (child reaped AND group empty) before `no_response` is raised; unconfirmable termination raises the distinct kind **`transport_unresolved`**; no retry, no late return path. **Witness** (`live-demo/test_connector_transport.py`, stub helpers only): success path unaltered (content, provider model id, typed yield outcome, identify() verbatim), request fidelity exact, expiry at ~1.02 s against a 10 s blocked helper with the pid confirmed dead and reaped, no late outcome, unresolved branch witnessed — **ALL PASS, verified in Remmy's own run** (the builder's two consecutive passes + Remmy's). Ben was offered the weaker-guarantee shortcut (INDEX 192's alternative) and **chose the full build** (2026-11-23 ~13:58 MST). **With Vane for the adjustment's pass; the rerun waits on it.**
194. **VANE ON THE CONNECTOR TRANSPORT — ACCEPTED WITH TWO FINAL CORRECTIONS (2026-11-23):** `VANE-CONNECTOR-TRANSPORT-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwtr423y-20-a856, examined @ 8fd562a8). The design is sound in principle; everything else is accepted as implemented. **Both defects verified first-hand by Remmy:** (1) the wrapper grants a fresh full `adapter.timeout` while the adapter's absolute monotonic deadline (`_begin_deadline` → `self._deadline`) is already running — the subprocess bound must be the **remaining time to that deadline** (no launch if none remains), with a witness terminating at the original deadline after part of it elapsed pre-call; (2) a failed `_getpgid` becomes `pgid = None` and `_group_gone(None)` returns True — an uninspectable group counted as empty; with `start_new_session=True` the pgid **is** the subprocess PID, and any failure to establish identity/emptiness yields `transport_unresolved`, with a negative witness. Plus the documentation correction: `transport_unresolved` enters `AdapterFailure`'s documented vocabulary as a transport failure, not an adapter outcome. **Status: correction round in build; on her final pass she releases the scoped rerun.**
195. **TRANSPORT CORRECTIONS FOLDED IN + WITNESSED (2026-11-23):** both corrections and the documentation fix are in. (1) The wrapper's bound is now the **remaining time to the adapter's established deadline** (`adapter._deadline` − now, read at call time); if none remains, the helper is **never launched** and `no_response` is raised; the full-timeout fallback survives only for direct `_call` use outside any public operation, which the live flow never takes. (2) `pgid = proc.pid` by construction; the `_getpgid`→None→"gone" path is **deleted** (no `_getpgid` reference remains); `_group_gone` is True only on `ProcessLookupError`, and any other probe failure ends at `transport_unresolved`. (3) `AdapterFailure`'s docstring now documents `transport_unresolved` as a transport failure, not an adapter outcome (docstring-only change to `adapter.py`). **Witness: 24 checks ALL PASS, verified in Remmy's own run** — the new witnesses land exactly on her cases: termination at the **original** deadline (2.02 s from establishment, 1.02 s from call, against a 30 s configured timeout), the never-launched expired case (no pid marker), and unestablishable emptiness raising `transport_unresolved` while the helper really was killed. Piece 8 adapter + A3 loop suites re-run green (the docstring change broke nothing). **Resubmitted for her final examination; her pass releases the rerun.**
196. **VANE'S TRANSPORT RE-EXAMINATION — BOTH FINDINGS CLOSED; ONE FINAL TIMING CORRECTION (2026-11-23):** `VANE-CONNECTOR-TRANSPORT-REEXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwtra382-21-6vle, examined @ 8fb9938e). The remaining-deadline and process-group corrections are **accepted**, as is the documentation fix. **One gap remains, verified first-hand by Remmy:** `bound` is computed before `Popen(...)` and passed stale to `communicate(timeout=bound)` — the deadline can expire during process creation itself, letting the helper run past it. Fix: recompute the remainder **after** `Popen`, before `communicate`; expired → never enter `communicate`, terminate + confirm through the same procedure (unconfirmed → `transport_unresolved`); plus a witness where creation consumes the deadline. **Status: final correction in build; on its pass the adjustment is approved and the scoped rerun proceeds.**
197. **FINAL TIMING CORRECTION FOLDED IN + WITNESSED (2026-11-23):** the wrapper now **recomputes the remaining time immediately after `Popen`**, before any wait: expired → `communicate` is never entered and the just-launched helper goes through the same terminate-and-confirm path (now factored into one shared `_expire_and_raise` used by both expiry paths, so they cannot diverge); remaining → `communicate` receives only the recomputed remainder, never the stale bound. **Witness: 30 checks ALL PASS, verified in Remmy's own run** — the new test 8 makes process creation consume the deadline (patched slow creation, 1.0 s span): termination completed at 1.52 s from the deadline, the helper never waited on past it, pid confirmed dead, no outcome, no late result. Nothing in `src/` or `tests/` changed in this round. **Resubmitted for Vane's final examination; her approval releases the scoped rerun.**
198. **VANE ON THE TIMING WITNESS — IMPLEMENTATION ACCEPTED; TEST 8 TOO WEAK TO PROVE IT (2026-11-23):** `VANE-CONNECTOR-TRANSPORT-WITNESS-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwtrfr1c-22-edd2, inspected @ b9c7e5be). The post-creation recompute + shared cancellation path are **accepted by inspection** — no implementation change asked. **Her catch, verified first-hand by Remmy:** test 8's only timing assertion allows span + 4.0 s (5.0 s on a 1.0 s span), and the original defect's timeline (~2.5 s: slow creation + stale-bound wait + termination) passes inside it — the test cannot distinguish fixed from defective. Required: an **instrumented process wrapper** making `communicate`'s entry (or non-entry) directly observable, asserting it is **never entered** when creation returns after the deadline; pid-dead and no-late-result checks retained. **Status: witness-strength correction in build; no rerun approval yet.**
199. **TEST 8 STRENGTHENED — DETERMINISTIC BOUNDARY SWEEP, ALL PASS (2026-11-23):** test 8 now proves the fact directly instead of timing it. An instrumented process wrapper records every `communicate` entry; the headline assertion is **zero body-carrying entries** when creation returns after the deadline (the one legitimate entry — the post-termination drain, no input, 2.0 s — is accounted for explicitly; under the stale-bound defect the body-carrying list would hold ~1.0 s). On top of that, a **controlled-clock sweep** (the method traces to **Ben's question** — whether the exact time could be determined rather than guessed at with a 0.5 s timer — with the worked-out method relayed back through him): a `_monotonic` alias seam in the wrapper (the one implementation-adjacent change; behaviour identical under the real clock; the termination windows deliberately stay on the real clock) lets the witness place creation delays of **0.4 / 0.8 / 1.0 / 1.2 s** against a 1.0 s span — communicate entered iff the remainder was positive, the recorded timeout **equals the computed remainder** to float precision, and at remainder exactly 0.0 and at −0.2 the wait count is ZERO. One real-clock companion retains the physical proof (real kill, reaped pid, no late result) with its timing assertion now honestly labelled secondary. **Witness: 48 checks ALL PASS, verified in Remmy's own run.** Resubmitted for Vane's final examination of the witness.

201. ⭐ **FIRST GENERATED-WORKER RUN — ATTEMPT 2 COMPLETED: THE WORKER DECLINED (2026-11-23):** the full composition ran end-to-end through the real provider via the approved connector transport — staging + manifest verification passed, commission, case issued, full A1 assembly to activation, per-instance onboarding with a real identity declaration ("I'm Kimi, an AI assistant developed by Moonshot AI…"), the bounded loop live. **Outcome: DECLINE at iteration 2 of 12, zero dispatches, no brief.** The cause, from the record: in iteration 1 the worker requested the tool **`read_file`** three times (once per source); the sealed grants name the registry tools `sandbox.read` / `sandbox.write`, so the doorway refused all three under R44, each refusal recorded. 205. ⭐ **PIECE A3-CAT BUILT (2026-11-23)** — verified first-hand by Remmy (sizes, the version in both copies, the divergence kinds; A3-CAT/A2/A3 suites ALL PASS in her own runs). Both registry copies restructured into immutable per-version views (`_TOOLS_V1` verbatim, `_TOOLS_V2` + worker-facing layer) under `tool-registry-v2`; a dispatch verifies under its recorded version, unknown versions keep `dispatch_registry_version_mismatch` with no double judgement, v1 dispatches never reinterpreted. `loop._build_package` derives the catalogue from the doorway's own `_generated_payload` (first grant per tool, mirroring the doorway's lookup; entries sorted, carrying exactly tool/description/parameters + the grant's own ceiling/bound/class as copies); unregistered grants advertise nothing; a registered-but-undescribed tool raises inside construction → attributable `LOOP_STALLED` runtime_failure, no iteration, no call. Every package carries `tools`; `LOOP_ITERATION` records `catalogue_hash` (canonical hash of the catalogue itself) + `registry_version`. The checker reconstructs independently (`catalogue_registry_version_unknown` / `catalogue_not_derivable` / `catalogue_hash_mismatch` — inability to verify is a divergence, never a silent ok). The adapter renders the catalogue verbatim in its own section (the wake dump omits the key so it appears once); tools-less prompts byte-identical. **Suite 22 checks incl. 211. ⭐ **FIRST-WORKER ATTEMPT 3 — THE CATALOGUE WORKS LIVE; THE RUN STALLS ON PROVIDER TIME (2026-11-23):** finished in 232 s, 31 events. **The worker used the catalogue**: iteration 1 requested `sandbox.read` three times under its real name (loop-assigned request ids, fingerprints recorded), **3 dispatches, 3 settled results** — all three sources read. Both LOOP_ITERATIONs carry the catalogue hash (`cffbd62f…`) + `tool-registry-v2`. **Iteration 2 — the composition call, with all three source texts in context — received no provider response within the call's 120 s total deadline**; the transport terminated it at the deadline as designed and the loop recorded `LOOP_STALLED` (ground `provider_failure`, "no_response: total deadline exceeded", exactly 120.0 s) after dispatch accounting closed (3/3 settled). **No brief** — composition competence remains untested, this time because the provider did not answer, not because the worker could not act. **Checker: all 11 checks ok** over the 31-event record. Identity: a fresh Kimi/Moonshot declaration. The open question the run leaves: **call budgeting** — the per-call span (120 s) bound long before the activation budget (600 s); whether a composition call deserves a larger per-call span is a design question for the examination, not a harness tweak. Two operational incidents ride in this entry's honesty: the first launch died at step zero on Remmy's compound-command path error (the watcher alone pushed "waiting"; **Ben caught it from his phone**), and the wall feed's placer-path bug (staging and placing disagreed on the placer's folder — **also surfaced by Ben's screen**) kept his wall on attempt 2's ghost until fixed mid-run and verified on his machine.
