## Working rules (Ben, 2026-11-21)

- **Work from this folder.** Remmy and Vane both work from `collaboration-framework/` — documents are written here, examined from here, and committed from here.
- **Date everything added.** Every file added to this folder carries its date (in the filename or a date line in the document).
- **Keep this folder updated as we go** (Ben, 2026-11-21 11:57 MST) — this index and the folder's contents are brought current in the same round as any change, never after.

## Structure (created 2026-11-21, shelf commit 916b3153)

The template layout stands inside this folder: `docs/` (vision.md; requirements/ — the SPEC SHEET lives here, current Revision 5 (approved 2026-11-21); architecture/, api/, decisions/ — ADR-001 records the substrate decision), `src/` (frontend / backend / shared), `tests/` (unit / integration / e2e / fixtures), `scripts/`, `infra/`, `.github/workflows/`, plus README.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, and .env.example. Plan Stage 2 (build the core) opened with Ben's design ruling of 2026-11-21; directories not yet built into still hold placeholders. The first build piece — the substrate carry-in at the ADR-002 pin (shelf commit 87c9ec08) — was carried into `src/fabric/` and `src/observer/` on 2026-11-22 (item 26).

220. **RESEARCH NOTE — HOW OTHERS HANDLE TIMEOUTS/DEADLINES (2026-11-23, at Ben's ask):** `RESEARCH-TIMEOUT-PATTERNS-2026-11-23.md` — a sourced comparison (gRPC/Google SRE deadline propagation; Temporal's four timeouts + heartbeat; Step Functions; DBOS deadline inheritance; LangGraph per-node run/idle timeouts; OpenAI Agents per-attempt timeout; Vercel AI SDK total/step/chunk timeouts; Mastra total+step; MCP progress-reset timers; OTel GenAI stage timing). Headlines: one propagated deadline with remaining-time inheritance is the established distributed-systems pattern (stacked independent timeouts are the documented failure mode); the heartbeat/idle-timeout family kills on silence, not duration — the mature cousin of Ben's span-as-observer; **no LLM framework runs a pure run-level deadline with per-call durations merely observed** — the field pairs a total with enforced sub-caps or progress signals; stage timing (TTFT + per-token) is standard observability, with provider internals unexposed (our "unknown" labels match). Discussion input for the attempt-5 configuration examination; changes nothing settled.

235. **ATTEMPT 6 EXAMINED — SETTLEMENT: STRUCTURED, AGENT-DIRECTED RETRIEVAL (2026-11-23):** `VANE-ATTEMPT6-EXAMINATION-RETRIEVAL-SETTLEMENT-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwtxu0k5-2k-7utr). Attempt 6's facts accepted (provider rejection; the route fast, the envelope refused). **The remedy is structured retrieval, not package slimming** — slimming treats the symptom and preserves the pattern. Her formulation of the defect: the worker chooses which files to read but **not how much it retrieves**; a read returns the whole document and the result enters the next request in full — "that is not the pull model Ben described." The worker must be able to discover material without its contents, inspect structure, request a section or bounded range, search within permitted material, and decide successive retrievals from what it has learned — with **search results and metadata under the same boundary as content** (no side door around the manifest). **Attempt 7 is NOT released under the unchanged run scope**; a narrow retrieval scope is required (discovery, bounded results, successive requests, manifest enforcement on every operation, provenance, no whole-document injection, whole-collection-avoidance tests). The three sources and the assignment stay intact. A first implementation of Ben's direction — **not** the A4 build. Her conclusion: the worker shouldn't need to swallow the library to find one useful paragraph.

The director's direction and the examiner's settlement (INDEX 235) converge on the same remedy in the same minutes, independently: structured, agent-directed retrieval, pulled forward ahead of attempt 7. Three retrieval tools in a new `tool-registry-v3` (both registry copies; v1/v2 frozen): `sandbox.list` (structure map, no contents), `sandbox.read_range` (a section or line range under a **hard 12,000-byte result ceiling** — oversized requests refused with the bound named, never silently truncated), `sandbox.search` (labelled passages within permitted material, same ceiling). `sandbox.read` refuses oversized results on the same terms, so whole-document injection becomes impossible through any door. Every operation — metadata included — resolves against the sealed grants and evidence boundary. Results enter the package as exactly what was pulled, with provenance labels; retrieval outcomes record parameters + the returned content's sha256 and size. 239. **RETRIEVAL SCOPE REV 2 — RESUBMITTED (2026-11-23):** the four corrections folded in. (1) **Aggregate ceiling: 36,000 bytes** of retrieved content per package (a named design bound), accounted from the record (settled retrieval outcomes since the previous package) and **enforced pre-execution at the doorway bounds check** — a request is admitted only if aggregate-so-far + the per-result ceiling ≤ the aggregate ceiling; refusals are recorded with the bound and remaining allowance named; the checker recomputes the accounting. 242. **RETRIEVAL SCOPE REV 3 EXAMINED — NOT PASSED; THREE IMPLEMENTATION-CRITICAL AMBIGUITIES (2026-11-23):** `VANE-RETRIEVAL-SCOPE-REV3-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwty7iby-2o-h4ve). The Rev 3 corrections are present; the direction is right. **Ambiguities:** (1) the aggregate accounting reads *settled* outcomes — requests admitted concurrently against the same total could collectively overrun it; required: **serialized dispatch** (each outcome settles before the next admission check) or atomic reservation, with **the checker verifying the accounting including outstanding/abandoned state**; (2) the boundary text contradicts the scratch grant — **two authority paths** (source: grant + boundary membership; scratch: its own sealed grant + designated path, never source evidence), **doorway-enforced**, the distinction preserved in the recorded authority provenance the checker verifies; (3) inward UTF-8 adjustment at both ends can **strand a character in neither slice** — a deterministic assignment rule and an R3 **multibyte fixture** proving byte-for-byte reconstruction are required. 243. **RETRIEVAL SCOPE REV 4 — RESUBMITTED (2026-11-23):** the three ambiguities closed. (1) **Serialized admission**: requests from one model response are dispatched one at a time, each outcome settling before the next admission check; an **uncertain** retrieval counts its full ceiling against the window; stall discipline closes accounting before any stall, so nothing dangles; the checker verifies each window's total (settled sizes + uncertain at ceiling) ≤ the aggregate ceiling. (2) **Two authority paths**, doorway-enforced: sources require grant + sealed boundary membership; `scratch/` requires its separately sealed scratch grant alone and exempts no other path; every outcome records its admitting path and the checker fails any outcome whose basis mismatches its path's scope. (3) **Straddle assignment**: served start advances to the next character boundary (a start-straddling character belongs to the previous slice), served end **extends** to the next boundary (an end-straddling character belongs to this slice) — adjacent windows tile exactly; the maximum requestable window is the ceiling minus 3 so a served slice never exceeds the ceiling; R3 gains a multibyte fixture with a character deliberately straddling the boundary, reconstructed byte-for-byte.

It would answer Vane's frequency question (INDEX 266 Q1) by design rather than by argument. **Status: Ben's idea, banked; NOT yet carried to Vane and NOT a build direction — Remmy's closing question (record as his proposal and carry it to Vane as the director's contribution to her three questions) awaits his word.**

270. **⭐⭐ VANE EXAMINES BEN'S NATIVE-MODE STAGE PROPOSAL — SUPPORTED IN PRINCIPLE, WITH THE DISCIPLINE SPECIFIED (2026-11-23):** `VANE-NATIVE-MODE-STAGE-EXAMINATION-2026-11-23.md` (PB pb-mwu1s3w6-31-aogk). "Ben has found a much stronger direction here" — the framework adapts its interface to the assembled worker **without adapting its authority boundary**. Her specification: the probe as a recorded assembly-stage operation (after identity, before judgement/packing); a synthetic probe case with a small fixed catalogue of harmless test tools and **no real filesystem, shell, network, credentials, shelf or side effects**; raw responses captured, **calls observed, never dispatched**; bounded calls/tokens/time; outcome classes text-contract / native-catalogue / environment-tool-attempt plus mixed, malformed, refusal, provider failure; **requested vs provider-accepted vs executed distinguished** (execution never happens in the probe). **Frequency tightened:** consistency claims need a small bounded **versioned trial set** reported as N-of-M counts; results never inherited across model/provider/config changes. The translation boundary is non-negotiable (native call → adapter translation → doorway → adjudication → dispatch → execution → outcome); environment capabilities are never granted for being asked for. The probe establishes **only** observed interaction behaviour — not identity, competence, trustworthiness or permission; the examiner weighs its evidence, the mode never selects itself. A scope must prove the listed points incl. **fail-closed on missing/malformed/inconclusive probes** and **one negative acceptance case** (an ungranted native request in work, refused without execution). **Recommendation: develop the scope — a bounded addition to A1's lifecycle, no implementation or attempt implied.**

(2) LOOP_ITERATION does not show the call was **entered** — the pre-call exception needs an independently visible call boundary, mutually exclusive with it. **Rev 5 (same file):** the helper's raw path records a **phase observation** (`pre_response` / `response_received` + status); `no_response` survives only as `connection_failed_pre_response` on that evidence; the timeout class is withdrawn to `unresolved` (`timeout_after_send`); a mid-read body failure is **captured partial** (status + partial bytes + `body_complete: false`, completeness false); absence/ambiguity of phase evidence defaults to `unresolved`. And a loop-owned **CALL_ENTERED** event before the callable is invoked makes the three histories (pre-call terminal / entered-no-response / returned) distinguishable from the record alone, the pre-call terminals mutually exclusive with it. CALL_ENTERED's semantics are stated bindingly, with four doorway rules (loop actor only, existing iteration, at most once, mutually exclusive with pre-call terminals; post-call events refused while a disposition is owed). 342. **PACED REPETITION — RELEASE WITHHELD: THE PACING CLOCK MUST BE MONOTONIC (2026-11-24):** `VANE-NATIVE-OFFER-PACED-RELEASE-EXAMINATION-2026-11-24.md` (PB pb-mwuka33g-13-kwfs). The pacing correction is otherwise well targeted. The one blocker: the runner's default clock is wall-clock `time.time`, used for both elapsed calculation and start recording — a forward wall-clock jump could fake the 65-second interval; a backward jump over-waits; the fake-clock witnesses exercise neither. Required: a **monotonic clock for elapsed/pacing**, wall-clock retained for chronology and distinguished from the measurement, **monotonic start offsets bound into the trial hash**, and witnesses that wall-clock jumps cannot shorten the interval and that the runner does not proceed before the interval has actually elapsed. Correction dispatched; the five-trial release remains unspent.
343. **RESEARCH PAUSE — NATIVE TOOL-USE RESEARCH DIGEST (2026-11-24):** `NATIVE-TOOL-USE-RESEARCH-2026-11-24.md` (the full sourced report, from the research run Ben ordered when he paused the loop). Headlines: the unoffered-tool reach is a **documented, benchmarked failure mode** (tool hallucination; *The Reasoning Trap* — reasoning/agentic training proportionally increases it; tool-calling is prediction, not registry lookup); **the exact refusal is attested on the same model family + provider** by independent practitioners (tools as prompt text → "it tried to press its own button instead of writing it as plain text"); gpt-oss's Harmony format makes tool calls a native channel (commentary) with browser/python as its trained built-ins — `container.exec` is a real ChatGPT tool but **no source shows gpt-oss was trained with it** (the name's origin is speculation; the reach is the affordance); a 14-model study found text tools better overall **but gpt-oss-120b specifically worse with text (−6.4pp)** — "taking the schema away removes the thing they were optimised for"; the providers' own prescribed pattern is native offering + host-side checked execution (OpenAI's flow, Groq's local tool calling); Groq enforces `tool_choice=none` by wholesale rejection, and catching unoffered calls in our own resolver (an error result the model can learn from) is the documented improvement over a provider 400. "Unnatural" as a literal claim is unmeasured; its evidenced form (distribution mismatch for this pinned model) is well supported, not by a controlled study of this exact setup. Carried to Vane as the loop's shared insight; the paced run stays paused.

Monotonic clock end to end (re-measure-and-re-wait loop; wall clock kept for chronology only, fields split and both hash-bound: `request_started_wall_at` + `request_start_monotonic_offset_seconds`); four new witnesses (wall +600 s forward jump ignored; −3600 s backward jump ignored; early-return sleeper re-waits, 28 sleeps; artifact reconstruction + hash binding). Suite now **35 checks ALL PASS (Remmy re-ran and counted)**; comparison still byte-identical (`cmp`); no live calls. 345. **VANE'S RESPONSE TO THE RESEARCH — ASSESSMENT MATERIALLY CHANGED; NATIVE-FIRST FAVOURED, AUTHORITY UNMOVED (2026-11-24):** `VANE-RESEARCH-RESPONSE-NATIVE-FIRST-2026-11-24.md` (PB pb-mwukkf1b-14-cbpl). Central conclusion: native tool expression is the model's normal request language; the framework retains complete authority over what happens next. (1) The text contract was fighting this model's training — text-only is now, in her assessment, a poor default **for this model and task shape** (not universally); the no-controlled-study qualification stands. (2) **Native presentation ≠ native authority**: the call is a structured request; resolution against the sealed catalogue, validation, grant/bounds/policy checks, doorway-only dispatch, full recording, results by call ID — "the provider's tool schema is an interface description, not an authority document." (3) Unoffered calls should become an **explicit doorway decision**: provider returns the call, our resolver rejects + records, then returns a narrow factual error — never dispatched for being returned; `disable_tool_validation` worth investigating within its limits. (4) `failed_generation` salvage stays a fallback, never the foundation, and only ever via translation into a typed request through the same doorway. (5) Two questions kept separate — **Q1 empirical** (provider reliably returns native calls for granted tools under the exact configuration) and **Q2 architectural** (adapter translates every call into the existing contract with no second authority path); she favours a native-first adapter with a closed-world resolver, not yet a build. The paced repetition's release requirement stands, unauthorised; she examines the clock correction next, then decides whether that experiment is still the right next measurement. Her line of record: **"adapt the framework's interface to the model, not the framework's authority to the model."** No build, scope, or request authorised.
346. **OPENCLAW RESEARCH (2026-11-24):** `OPENCLAW-RESEARCH-2026-11-24.md` (full sourced report, Ben's ask alongside INDEX 343). OpenClaw (Steinberger; Nov 2025 as Clawdbot → Moltbot → OpenClaw; foundation-run since July 2026) is a self-hosted always-on agent runtime, not an SDK. Findings of record for the framework: it uses **providers' native tool calling**, with tools **policy-filtered before the model sees them** (8-layer pipeline, deny wins) and three distinct controls — tool policy / sandbox / **host-side exec approvals** (stricter-of-two, bound to canonical command+path, allow-once default, fail-closed) — independent corroboration of the native-first + doorway shape. **Its defaults invert its hardening** (sandbox off, host exec full/no-ask, agent-to-agent allow-all, secrets unencrypted at rest); the security record is heavy (CVSS 8.8 one-click RCE; tens of thousands of exposed gateways; ~12% of audited marketplace skills malicious in one audit; the canonical case of a prompt-carried "don't act until I tell you" lost to context compaction, 200+ emails deleted — approval in a prompt is not a gate, the negative proof of our record/doorway principle). Multi-agent is delegation (spawn/send, depth caps, per-agent workspaces, credentials unshared) with **no work-state/approval-authority separation** — approval exists only for host exec; hierarchies are prompt conventions. Adopt-list: policy-filtered offering, host-side approval interlock details, spawn discipline, audit-as-feature. Hedges: late-2026 adoption figures are secondary estimates; several CVE tallies conflict by counting method.
347. **PACED RELEASE — CLOCK PASSES; ONE MEASUREMENT-BOUNDARY DEFECT REMAINS (2026-11-24):** `VANE-PACED-CLOCK-EXAMINATION-MEASUREMENT-BOUNDARY-2026-11-24.md` (PB pb-mwukoz6w-15-fb0g). Required: move the monotonic start to **immediately before the `raw_call` invocation, after preparation**; add a **variable-preparation-delay witness** proving the spacing at the call boundary; describe the recorded wait accurately (wait-phase start → call-boundary timestamp). Release held; **the paced experiment remains, in her words, the right next measurement**; the five-trial release stays unspent. Correction dispatched.

354. **NATIVE ADAPTER — SCOPE PAPER REV 1 DRAFTED; SUBMITTED FOR EXAMINATION (2026-11-24):** `NATIVE-ADAPTER-SCOPE-2026-11-24.md` (51 requirements, N1–N51; open questions OQ1–OQ8), drafted at Ben's tap (04:06 MST) on Vane's INDEX 345/353 boundary, verified by Remmy at receipt (structure, requirement and question counts).
